# Project Learnings — vscode-quarto-ext

**What this is:** the append-only table of project-specific learnings accumulated at each
session's Phase 3C close-out (`SESSION_RUNNER.md` §3C). Extracted here from `CLAUDE.md` on
2026-07-09 (Session 39) because the table had grown to ~97% of that file's size (159 KB of
164 KB, 45 rows) and `CLAUDE.md` is loaded in full into every session's context — this file
is not. `CLAUDE.md` → Project-Specific Methodology Adaptations → Project-specific Learnings
now carries a plain Markdown link pointer to this file (deliberately not an `@`-import, which
would auto-expand into context every session and defeat the purpose of moving this out).

**This file is project-owned** — committed at the project root like `CLAUDE.md` itself, never
touched by `bin/sync` (that only touches `SESSION_RUNNER.md`, `SAFEGUARDS.md`,
`docs/methodology/**`, and other byte-identical canonical copies). No tooling changes were
needed to adopt it.

**How to use it:**
- **At Phase 3C (Document Learnings):** append a new row to the bottom of the table below with
  the next sequential number. Do not add rows back to `CLAUDE.md`.
- **At Phase 0 / when starting work in a previously-touched area:** grep this file for relevant
  keywords (a phase name, a file path, a feature name) rather than reading it in full — it will
  keep growing, exactly as it did inside `CLAUDE.md`.

| # | Learning | When to apply |
|---|----------|---------------|
| 1 | **Posit's official Quarto VS Code extension is AGPL-3.0** (extension, LSP, Visual Editor, Panmirror — confirmed twice against the live `quarto-dev/quarto` repo). It is **look-but-don't-copy** reference only. Build on MIT upstreams instead: `microsoft/vscode-markdown-tm-grammar`, `wooorm/markdown-tm-language`, `microsoft/vscode-markdown-languageservice`, and the MIT Quarto **CLI** (shelled out at runtime). | Before reusing/adapting any Quarto-extension code, or choosing a grammar/LSP base. The MIT boundary in CONTEXT.md is non-negotiable. |
| 2 | **Language-support architecture is resolved: ship Tier A (TextMate grammar) → build to Tier B (in-process `register*Provider`) → defer Tier C (out-of-process LSP).** The guardrail that makes this safe: the intelligence core is a **`vscode`-free pure-TS library**; the extension is a thin adapter. This makes B→C migration cheap AND makes the core unit-testable headlessly. See `docs/planning/2026-06-27-extension-architecture-plan.md` §3. | Any language-feature work. Keep parsing/analysis logic out of `vscode.*` imports. |
| 3 | **Automated runtime verification WORKS here — `@vscode/test-electron` confirmed in Phase 1 (Session 2).** Despite no `code` CLI on PATH, test-electron downloads its own VS Code (v1.126.0, ~261 MB into `.vscode-test/`, gitignored) and runs integration tests **headlessly**: it activates the extension in a real host and executes commands end-to-end (Phase 1's test runs `quarto.verifyInstallation` against the real CLI, exit 0). This is **stronger than manual F5** for wiring/activation/dispatch. Manual F5 remains the only way to visually confirm notification/UI text. Pure-`core/` logic is unit-tested with `npm test` (vitest, no VS Code). | Phase 3E for any implementation session: prefer `npm run test:integration` for runtime-behavior verification; reserve manual F5 for visual/UX confirmation. Never claim build-clean == runtime-clean — but you can now usually *prove* runtime-clean automatically. |
| 4 | **Verified Quarto CLI behaviors (trust these):** `quarto --version` prints just the bare semver (`1.7.33\n`, no banner); `quarto preview <f>` prints `Browse at http://localhost:<port>/` (parse this for the preview webview); `--timeout` does NOT reliably self-exit (only on no active clients → own the process lifecycle, kill on close); code-cell render needs Jupyter (`nbformat`) in the active Python env. | Phases 3–5 (render/preview/execution). Re-verify the `Browse at` line on Quarto CLI upgrades. |
| 5 | **Phase 1 build toolchain (working — trust these).** Two compilers by design: esbuild bundles `src/extension.ts` → `dist/extension.js` (`vscode` external); a SEPARATE `tsc -p tsconfig.test.json` compiles `test/integration/**` → `out/` for test-electron to run. vitest is scoped to `test/unit/**` so it never picks up the mocha integration tests. **Gotchas caught in Phase 1:** `.vscodeignore` MUST exclude `.claude/**` and `.git/**` (else they leak into the `.vsix`); `npm run package` needs `--allow-missing-repository` and README must avoid relative links (e.g. `./LICENSE`) until a git remote + `repository` field exist. | Any session that compiles/tests/packages. When a git remote is added: drop `--allow-missing-repository`, add `repository` to package.json, and the README relative-link restriction lifts. |
| 6 | **Phase 2 grammar — base-grammar question RESOLVED by NOT forking.** `syntaxes/quarto.tmLanguage.json` (scope `text.html.quarto`) is **original work** that `include`s VS Code's built-in `text.html.markdown` **by scope-name reference** (no source copied) for prose/plain fences, and adds only the Quarto-specific rules: a `\A`-anchored YAML front-matter region (→`source.yaml`) and brace-cell rules ` ```{python|r|julia|ojs} ` (→`source.python`/`source.r`/`source.julia`/`source.js`). This beats forking `markdown-tm-language`/`vscode-markdown-tm-grammar` (nothing large to copy/attribute; markdown stays current) and is the canonical `mjbvz` injection pattern (MIT). Each cell's content is `contentName: meta.embedded.block.<lang>`, mapped to a language in `package.json` → `contributes.grammars.embeddedLanguages` (this mapping, not the grammar, is what enables bracket-match/comment-toggle inside cells). A `cell-generic` fallback scopes any other `{lang}`. **Caveat:** `source.r`/`source.julia` are NOT bundled with VS Code — those cells get the fenced + `meta.embedded.block.*` scope but only colorize if the user has the R/Julia extension installed (python/js always colorize). Attribution is in `/NOTICE` (licensing hard gate). | Any grammar/highlighting work, or adding a new cell language (add a `cell-<lang>` rule + an `embeddedLanguages` entry + a `tokenize.test.ts` assertion). |
| 7 | **Headless grammar verification works — automate token scopes, don't defer to F5.** `test/unit/tokenize.test.ts` loads the grammar into the SAME engines VS Code uses (`vscode-textmate` + `vscode-oniguruma`, both MIT devDeps) and tokenizes the fixture, asserting `meta.embedded.block.*` scopes + the discriminating cases (a plain ` ```python ` block and post-cell prose must NOT be in a cell). This is the automated equivalent of "Developer: Inspect Editor Tokens and Scopes" and catches regex bugs (back-referenced closing fence, `\A` anchor, `\b` boundaries) the structural JSON guard cannot. **Load-bearing gotcha:** in the standalone test registry, `loadGrammar` MUST return an empty stub `{scopeName, patterns: []}` for unresolved external includes (markdown/yaml/source.*), **NOT `null`** — returning null silently corrupts vscode-textmate's pattern compilation (sibling rules stop matching / reorder), which cost most of Phase 2's debugging. Real VS Code always has those grammars, so the extension itself is unaffected. **Fixture trick:** `#| eval: false` on `{python}`/`{r}`/`{julia}` cells lets `quarto render` validate the doc (exit 0) WITHOUT needing Jupyter/R/Julia kernels. | Verifying any grammar (prefer this over manual F5 for scopes — extends Learning #3; F5 remains only for theme-dependent COLOR). Writing render-clean `.qmd` fixtures (Phases 3/5). |
| 8 | **`quarto render` I/O contract (verified live 1.7.33 — extends Learning #4).** Progress, the `Output created: <path>` success marker, AND error text all go to **STDERR**; STDOUT is empty. So you CANNOT distinguish success from failure by stream — **key off the exit code** (0 = success, non-zero = failure; missing-Jupyter is exit 1 with the `nbformat` traceback verbatim on stderr). The reported output path is **relative to the input file's directory** (resolve against `dirname(file)`), and the marker line may be wrapped in **ANSI SGR escapes** (`\x1b[..m`) — strip them before parsing. Stream BOTH stdout+stderr to the Output channel; `core/render-args.ts` keeps `buildRenderArgs` + `parseOutputPath` pure/testable. | Phase 4 preview (also parses a stderr line — `Browse at`), Phase 5 execution, or any CLI-wrapper feature. Capture the real stream layout live before coding the parser. |
| 9 | **Faithful-verification trap in the test-electron host (gate d / FM #24): the host resolves a DIFFERENT Python than your interactive shell.** Phase 3's missing-Jupyter fixture (`needs-jupyter.qmd`, executable `{python}`, no `eval:false`) **fails via the CLI in this shell** (miniforge python, no `nbformat`) but **renders SUCCESSFULLY inside the downloaded VS Code host** (it resolves a Jupyter-capable python). An `assert.doesNotReject` failure-path test against it therefore passes **trivially** (a success also doesn't reject) — verifying nothing. Fix: for automated failure-path tests use an **environment-independent deterministic failure** (`render-error.qmd`: an invalid `format:` → exit 1 "Validation of YAML front matter failed" in ANY env, no kernel) and verify env-dependent degradation (missing Jupyter) **live via the CLI**. **Timing is a faithfulness signal:** the genuine failure runs in <1s; a sneaky success runs in ~4s (kernel+pandoc). | Phase 5 run-cell and any test of a degradation/error path whose trigger depends on the host environment (kernels, installed extensions, PATH). Don't trust a green "does-not-throw" test unless the failure it claims to exercise is environment-independent. |
| 10 | **Strict TDD is mandatory project-wide (operator directive, Session 5).** Every implementation/bug-fix task follows red → green → refactor, one test at a time, vertical slices (never all-tests-then-all-code). The binding statement and exact scope/exemptions are in the **§"Mandatory development practice — strict TDD"** subsection above; the `/tdd` skill is the operative how-to. | Start of any implementation/bugfix session — invoke `/tdd` and write the failing test before the code. Pure config/grammar/doc edits with no logic are exempt but still need their normal verification. |
| 11 | **`quarto preview` process-tree reaping + the deno-worker faithful-verification trap (Phase 4, verified live 1.7.33).** `quarto preview` is a **bash wrapper** (`/usr/local/bin/quarto preview …`) that spawns a long-lived **deno worker** whose cmdline reads **`…deno run … quarto.js preview …`**. Two load-bearing consequences: (a) **Killing the wrapper first REPARENTS the deno worker** (it survives, orphaned) — so spawn **`detached: true`** (the wrapper leads a process group) and signal the whole group atomically: `process.kill(-pid, "SIGTERM")` then, gated on a `process.kill(-pid, 0)` liveness probe, `"SIGKILL"`. A group outlives its leader while any member is alive, so the group signal still reaps a wrapper-died/worker-alive case. (b) **`pgrep -f "quarto preview"` matches the wrapper but NOT the deno worker** (`quarto.js preview` ≠ `quarto preview`) — so a `"quarto preview"` probe/`pkill` reports "clean" while the deno worker orphans (a gate-d trap; it bit my own capture script AND would have made the no-orphan test pass falsely). Use a fixture-scoped pattern that matches **both**: `pgrep -f "preview.*<fixture>"`. The `Browse at <url>` marker is on **STDERR** (stdout empty), ANSI-wrapped — Learning #8 extends to preview; pin the live-captured line. | Phase 5+ or any long-lived-CLI feature: spawn detached, group-kill, and write the no-orphan probe to match the real worker process, not the wrapper. Re-verify the wrapper/worker topology on a Quarto upgrade. |
| 12 | **Long-lived-process managers have check-then-act races across `await`s — and an adversarial review caught one the integration test missed (Phase 4).** The "one preview per document" guard read the sessions map, but the session was only registered **after** two awaits (`doc.save()`, `resolveBinary()` — which spawns `quarto --version`, uncached). Two rapid `quarto.preview` invocations both passed the guard and both spawned; the second's session overwrote the first in the map, so the first server was tracked by nothing and **orphaned permanently (surviving deactivate)** — defeating the whole no-orphan design. Fix: **reserve the slot synchronously in a `Set` before the first await** (try/finally to release). The single-invocation integration test was green and missed it; a 4-lens adversarial review (with adversarial verification of each finding) surfaced it + 4 lower-severity issues. Lesson: for any in-process manager keyed by an id, make check-and-claim atomic *before* the first await; and run an adversarial review over lifecycle/concurrency code — a passing happy-path test is not coverage of the race. | Any feature that tracks long-lived resources in a map/registry across async setup (Phase 5 run-cell sessions, future preview-format/port features). Reserve the key synchronously; add a concurrent-invocation regression test. |
| 13 | **Phase 5 delegated run-cell — three load-bearing facts (one a dead-on-arrival bug the integration suite masked).** (a) **`activationEvents: []` does NOT activate the extension when a `.qmd` is opened** — `contributes.languages`/`grammars` do not trigger activation; only the auto-generated `onCommand` does. So a keybinding gated on a context key the extension sets in `activate()` (here `quarto.inCodeCell`, gating `ctrl/shift+enter`) is **dead until the user first runs a palette command**. Fix: add **`onLanguage:quarto`** to `activationEvents`. The integration suite force-`activate()`s the extension in its `before()` hook, so it **masked** this — an adversarial review caught it; a real fresh-open (F5) or a `packageJSON.activationEvents` contribution-guard test is needed. (b) **Faithful delegated-dispatch testing = register a STAND-IN delegate command.** The clean `@vscode/test-electron` host has no Jupyter, so `vscode.commands.registerCommand("jupyter.execSelectionInteractive", …)` is free; have the stand-in capture the **selected text** at call time to prove the full chain (find-cell → select-code → invoke-delegate) and pin the selection math, env-independently (gate d; extends Learning #9). (c) **Run-cell runs the IN-EDITOR buffer (no `doc.save()`)** — unlike render/preview, which save because the CLI reads from disk; the delegated path's dependency is the user's **kernel + language extension**, not the Quarto CLI (distinct from Learning #4/#9's render-path Jupyter need). | Any feature gated on an extension-set context key, or expected to work on file-open before any command runs (→ add `onLanguage:`, and don't trust a suite that force-activates); testing any feature that delegates to an external command ID (→ stand-in command + assert captured side effects); deciding whether a CLI-vs-delegated feature must persist the buffer first. |
| 14 | **Phase 6a outline/symbols — the shared region model, and the "two scanners disagree" trap an adversarial review caught.** (a) **One region scanner, many views.** `core/qmd/model.ts` `scanRegions()` is the single linear pass that classifies every line (YAML front matter `---`…`---`, block HTML comment `<!-- -->`, code fence, ATX heading); `findHeadings`, `findAllCells`, `findCellAtPosition`, and `buildOutline` are thin views over it. **Phase 5's `core/cells.ts` was folded in** (it's now a re-export shim) so cell-finding has ONE implementation. The 17 Phase-5 cell tests are the consolidation regression net — keep them green. (b) **The trap:** Phase 6a first shipped `findHeadings` (front-matter- and fence-aware) and a SEPARATE `findAllCells` (fence-aware only). The two **disagreed on skip-regions** — `findAllCells` happily found a phantom cell inside YAML front matter / an HTML comment / a 4-space-indented fence (CommonMark §4.5 indented code), corrupting both the outline AND Phase 5 run-cell (which shares `findAllCells`). Happy-path tests (97 unit / 25 integration, all green) missed all of it; a 5-lens adversarial review with refute-by-default verification found 8 confirmed, fixed 6 by **unifying into `scanRegions`** (front matter + comments + a 0–3-space fence-indent cap matching the ATX rule), plus stripping the Pandoc `{#sec-id .class}` heading-attribute block from the display name (ubiquitous Quarto construct; the `#sec-` id will drive Phase 6b cross-refs — store it structurally then). (c) **Faithful, env-independent provider test:** `DocumentSymbolProvider` is verified via `vscode.executeDocumentSymbolProvider` in the real test-electron host — no Quarto CLI / Jupyter needed (strongest verification here; extends Learnings #3/#9). Registering a provider needs **no `package.json` contribution** and isn't gated on a context key, so the Learning #13a dead-on-arrival activation trap does not recur. (d) **Deferred (documented scope, not a bug):** setext headings (`===`/`---` underlines) — the model is ATX-only; setext needs `---` disambiguation vs thematic break / front matter and deserves its own TDD pass (backlog). | Any feature consuming the `.qmd` structure (6b cross-refs, 6c citations): consume `scanRegions`/`buildOutline`, don't write a third scanner. When you add a parser that overlaps an existing one, assert they AGREE on skip-regions (front matter, comments, fences) or you'll ship a phantom. Verify any provider via `vscode.execute*Provider` in the host. Lead any model change with a failing unit test (strict TDD). |
| 15 | **Phase 6b cross-references — three label sources over one scanner, two completion-provider traps, and the jupyter-engine render trap.** (a) **One scanner, three sources.** `core/refs.ts` `indexLabels` builds the label index entirely from `findHeadings` (heading `{#sec-…}` ids — `Heading.id`, previously parsed-and-discarded, now kept), `findAllCells` (`#|`/`//|` `label:` cell options), and `findBodyLines` (inline `{#fig-/tbl-/eq-/lst-…}` on prose/heading lines) — all views over `scanRegions` (Learning #14). It writes NO line logic of its own. The Learning-#14 **agreement test** (label-like text in front matter / comments / fences must NOT be indexed) is the guard, and it immediately caught a real **single-line `<!-- … -->` comment leak** (only block comments were skipped before) — fix it in the shared scanner, not in refs. (b) **Two provider traps a happy-path suite misses (caught by adversarial review):** the cross-ref **completion + definition providers fire EVERYWHERE in the `.qmd`** (selector `{language:"quarto"}`; embedded TextMate scopes don't reroute providers) — so `@` in a `{python}`/`{julia}` cell pops the list (decorator/macro) and go-to-def resolves a `@fig-` in cell string literals. **Gate both on a prose predicate** (`isReferenceableLine` = `findBodyLines` membership; includes headings, excludes cells/front-matter/comments). And a single completion `range` from `@`→cursor **duplicates the suffix** when accepting mid-token — use an `{inserting, replacing}` range where `replacing` reaches the token END (forward-scan past the cursor). (c) **The jupyter-engine render trap (extends Learnings #4/#7/#9):** a **python-only** `.qmd` with per-cell `#| eval: false` STILL fails `quarto render` in this shell (no `nbformat`) — the **jupyter engine starts the kernel regardless of per-cell `eval:false`**. `sample.qmd` only renders clean because its `{r}` cell selects the **knitr** engine. For a render-clean python-only fixture, set doc-level **`execute:\n  enabled: false`** in the front matter. (d) **Deferred (documented, not a bug in handled input):** inline `{#fig-…}` inside a CommonMark §4.4 **indented (4-space) code block** is a phantom — a faithful fix must not false-skip 4-space list-item continuation content (model tracks no list context), so it needs its own list-aware TDD pass (backlog). | Phase 6c citations (reuse `crossrefCompletionContext` + `isReferenceableLine`; a bare `@key` is detected/gated the same way) and any provider work: gate completion/definition to the right regions (a whole-doc language selector fires inside cells), and build replace ranges that cover the whole token. Any `.qmd` structure consumer: consume the shared scanner; assert agreement on skip-regions. Any `{python}`-cell test fixture: make it render-clean with doc-level `execute: enabled: false`. |
| 16 | **Phase 6c citations — the front-matter reader, the "don't reuse a token-scanner across token grammars" trap, and parser/adapter robustness.** (a) **The region model skips front matter, so reading `bibliography:` needs its own reader** — `core/frontmatter.ts` `bibliographyPaths` is a focused, no-YAML-lib reader for the one key, handling scalar / flow-list / block-list forms. A minimal hand reader is right for v1, BUT the adversarial review found 4 real YAML-edge defects it first missed: a **zero-indent block list** (`bibliography:` then `- a.bib` flush-left — valid YAML) parsed to `[]`; a **trailing `# comment`** was kept as part of the path (and defeated flow-list detection); an empty quoted scalar returned `['']`. Real front matter is messier than the happy path — test comments, CRLF, indentation variants. (b) **THE BIG TRAP — do not reuse a token-scanner built for a different token grammar.** 6c first reused `crossrefCompletionContext`/`ID_CHAR` (`[A-Za-z0-9_-]`) for citekeys, but **citekeys routinely contain `:`/`.`** (biblatex `Knuth:1984`, DBLP `DBLP:journals/...`, dotted `einstein.1905`). Result: completion **stopped firing after a `:`** and **duplicated the suffix on a mid-token accept** (`@Knuth:1984` → `@Knuth:1984:1984`) — the EXACT finding-E class Learning #15(b) claimed eliminated, silently reintroduced by reuse. Fix: a citation-specific `citationCompletionContext` with a citekey char class (`[A-Za-z0-9_:.+/-]`, trailing-punct-trimmed). When you reuse a scanner/regex for a new token type, re-verify its character class matches the new grammar. (c) **Parser robustness the happy path misses (all 3/3-confirmed):** brace-matching must be **quote-aware** (a stray `{` inside a quoted BibTeX value else makes `matchBrace` consume/discard the rest of the file); **strip a leading UTF-8 BOM before `JSON.parse`** (it throws on a BOM → a BOM-saved CSL-JSON silently loads zero citations; BibTeX via `indexOf("@")` is BOM-immune, so the bug is CSL-only); accept `(`/`)`-delimited entries; type-guard a CSL `date-parts` leaf (`[[null]]` → `year:"null"`). (d) **Faithful tests for layered-defense adapter branches (gate d):** the "missing bib / no bibliography / non-file scheme" safety paths are layered, so no single behavior test isolates one guard — establish discrimination by **temporarily breaking each guard, observing the targeted test go RED, then reverting** (done this session: the early-return break reds no-bib+untitled; a spurious-result break reds missing-bib). A green degradation test isn't faithful until you've seen it fail for the right reason. A fixture whose purpose is a missing bib **cannot be render-clean** (Quarto rejects it) — document that like `render-error.qmd`. | Any feature reading an external file named in front matter (reuse `bibliographyPaths`; resolve relative to `dirname(doc)`); **any reuse of an existing completion/token scanner for a different token type — check the char class first**; any new file-format parser (quote-aware brace matching, BOM strip, never throw on malformed input → `[]`); any adapter degradation path (prove the guard with a break-revert RED, pair positive/negative controls). |
| 17 | **v1 release-prep item 1 — wiring the GitHub remote (verified live, Session 10).** (a) **The repo `rmsharp/vscode_quarto_ext` pre-existed with GitHub auto-init** (a throwaway `LICENSE` + 1-line `README.md`) on an **UNRELATED history**, and its **default branch is `master`, not `main`** (operator's choice). Local `main` was renamed to `master` (`git branch -m`) and reconciled with **`git push -u origin master --force-with-lease`** — discarding the auto-init (we already have a real `LICENSE` + `README.md`). For any future remote wiring: **inspect the remote first** (`git ls-remote origin`, `git show --stat origin/<branch>`, `git merge-base` to detect unrelated histories); expect auto-init; reconcile by force-with-lease, never a junk `--allow-unrelated-histories` merge. (b) **vsce normalizes the repo's `LICENSE` (no extension) to `LICENSE.txt` INSIDE the `.vsix`**, but the working-tree file is `LICENSE` — so a README relative link must target **`LICENSE`** (not `LICENSE.txt`). The marketplace rewrites relative links to absolute GitHub URLs using the `repository` field, which is exactly why Learning #5's relative-link restriction only lifts once `repository` exists. (c) **Dropping `--allow-missing-repository` works the moment a `repository` field is in `package.json`** — vsce reads `package.json`, NOT the git remote, so packaging needs no push. Verified: clean **9-file** `.vsix` (19 KB) without the flag, 190/190 unit green. | The rest of v1 release prep — **item 2** (marketplace metadata: real `publisher`/`icon`/`keywords`/`bugs`/`homepage`, polished `displayName`/`description`, README rewrite) and **item 3** (F5 visual pass + screenshots) — and any future GitHub-remote wiring or marketplace publish. The remote is now wired (`origin` → `master`); `repository` is set; the `package` script is flag-free. |
| 18 | **v1 release-prep item 2 — marketplace metadata + README (verified live, Session 11).** (a) **THE TRAP: `publisher` (and `name`) are not just listing copy — they form the runtime extension ID `<publisher>.<name>`.** Changing `publisher` `vscode-quarto-ext` → `rmsharp` changed the ID to `rmsharp.vscode-quarto-ext`, which the integration suite **hard-codes** (`const EXTENSION_ID = "…"`, copy-pasted into all **8** `test/integration/suite/*.test.ts`). `npm run compile`, `npm run package` (clean `.vsix`), AND `npm test` (190/190 unit) ALL passed — only the full **`npm run test:integration` RED'd 8 "should be discoverable" `before all` failures** (the suite resolves the extension by ID in each file's setup hook). Lesson: **metadata that is also an identity is runtime-coupled — re-run the integration suite after any `publisher`/`name`/activation change; package+unit green is NOT sufficient** (extends Learning #13's "don't trust a force-activating suite"). The constant is duplicated → backlog item to extract/derive it from `package.json`. (b) **Icon: author an SVG, rasterize with `rsvg-convert -w 256 -h 256` (PIL 12 also present) → `media/icon.png`.** `.vscodeignore` excludes `media/**`? **No** — it lists specific dirs, none matching `media/`, and no `**/*.png` glob, so the icon ships; **confirm with `vsce ls`** (it must list `media/icon.png`). vsce passes PNGs through as-is (only `LICENSE`→`LICENSE.txt` is renamed, Learning #17). Art is **original** (a document card + `</>` + "MIT" badge), not Quarto's trademarked logo — the clean-room/licensing posture (Learning #1) extends to branding. `icon` must be ≥128×128; 256×256 used. (c) **README drafted via a judge-panel + accuracy-critic Workflow against a FIXED factual brief** (drafters told NOT to read the repo → no stale-content bleed; the accuracy critic refute-checks every claim against the brief). This de-staled the old status line ("execution + editor intelligence still to come" — all shipped Phases 5/6a–6c) with zero fabricated/overreaching claims. Relative links (`LICENSE`, `NOTICE`, the `#license--attribution` anchor — note GitHub's double-hyphen slug for "License & attribution") resolve and are marketplace-rewritten via `repository` (Learnings #5/#17). `preview: true` set as the honest 0.0.1 first-listing state. Verified: clean **10-file** `.vsix` (28.82 KB) with `media/icon.png` embedded, **190/190** unit + **42/42** integration green. | Any marketplace-metadata change — **especially `publisher`/`name`/activation: re-run `npm run test:integration`, not just package+unit** (the 8 `EXTENSION_ID` constants track the ID). Generating any shipped extension asset (rasterize + `vsce ls` to confirm inclusion; keep art original). Item 3 (F5 visual pass + screenshots → the `<!-- SCREENSHOTS: placeholder -->` slot in the README) and the `npm audit` decision remain; actual `vsce publish` needs a Marketplace account/PAT (operator step). |
| 19 | **v1 release-prep item 3 — the F5 visual pass + README screenshots, and its faithfulness trap (verified live, Session 12).** (a) **THE TRAP — the user's installed Posit Quarto extension (`quarto.quarto`) runs in the Extension Development Host alongside ours and MERGES with it.** F5 loads ALL installed extensions plus the one under `--extensionDevelopmentPath`, so Posit's grammar / outline / `@`-completion / `Quarto: Preview`+`Render` stack on ours — every screenshot then shows *its* UI or a merge, not ours, defeating the pass's whole purpose (faithfulness). **The tell: a `▷ Run Cell \| Run Next Cell` CodeLens above code cells is NOT ours** (we register zero CodeLens — `grep -ri codelens src/` is empty); it is Posit's. **Fix: launch the dev host with `--disable-extensions`** (add it to `.vscode/launch.json` `args`; it disables all *installed* extensions but KEEPS the `--extensionDevelopmentPath` one) → only our extension loads; revert the flag at close-out. (b) **Two-windows trap:** the dev host is a SEPARATE window from the project window — disabling Posit in the *normal* window does nothing to the host, and outline/commands act in whichever window is focused. Rule: only act where the file is colorized / the status bar reads `Quarto` (title contains `[Extension Development Host]`). (c) **Capture mechanics:** pasted screenshots land at `~/.claude/image-cache/<session-uuid>/<N>.png` (the path is printed in chat) — copy from THERE, not `~/Desktop` (macOS clipboard captures never hit disk). (d) **Packaging:** screenshots are kept OUT of the `.vsix` via `.vscodeignore` `media/screenshots/**` (the icon `media/icon.png` still ships); vsce rewrites relative README image paths to `https://github.com/<repo>/raw/HEAD/...` (verified by unzipping the packaged `readme.md`) → **the images must be committed AND pushed AND the repo must be PUBLIC** — those raw URLs 404 for anonymous requests on a private repo, so BOTH the Marketplace listing and the in-VS-Code Extensions view show broken images until the repo is public (or the PNGs are hosted at a public HTTPS URL). Verified live: all 5 URLs returned 404 while the repo was private → HTTP 200 the instant it was made public. (e) A render-clean showcase fixture needs no kernel via doc-level `execute: enabled: false` (extends Learning #15c). **This pass closes the "F5-only visual residue" every prior phase (5 / 6a–6c) carried** — highlighting, outline, `@`-completion (cross-ref + citation coexisting), live preview, and render were all eyeballed live in an isolated host. | Any future F5 / visual / screenshot / marketplace-asset pass: isolate the dev host with `--disable-extensions` (the user has Posit's `quarto.quarto` installed), act only in the `[Extension Development Host]` window, pull pasted shots from `~/.claude/image-cache/`, keep shots out of the `.vsix` but commit+push them, and make `{python}` fixtures render-clean with doc-level `execute: enabled: false`. |
| 20 | **v1 release-prep — the `npm audit` posture decision (Session 13).** (a) **What ships ≠ what `npm audit` scans.** A VS Code extension ships the **esbuild bundle** (`dist/extension.js`, `vscode` external) + static assets — **never `node_modules`** (confirm with `vsce ls`/the `npm run package` file tree). `npm audit` scans the whole dev dependency tree (here 487 deps). So a vuln only reaches a user if it is a **runtime `dependency` that gets bundled**. This project has **`"dependencies": {}`** (everything is a `devDependency`), so all 7 advisories (esbuild bundler, vitest unit runner, mocha integration runner + transitives vite/vite-node/@vitest/mocker/serialize-javascript) are **dev-only and unreachable by users**. **Verify "none ship" four ways, don't assert it:** `dependencies:{}` empty + `grep -rnE "<pkgs>" src/` clean + the shipped `dist/extension.js` contains none of the names + `vsce ls` shows no `node_modules`. (b) **`npm audit` exits 0 here regardless of findings** (npm's exit code keys off flags like `--audit-level`, not the presence of vulns) — never gate a script on its exit code; parse `npm audit --json` (`.metadata.vulnerabilities`, `.vulnerabilities[].nodes`). (c) **`npm audit fix --force` can be NET-NEGATIVE.** Plain `npm audit fix` (semver-safe) fixed **0 of 7** here (confirm with `--dry-run`); the `--force` path was breaking AND wrong — it would major-bump esbuild (0.24→0.28) & vitest (2→3) and **DOWNGRADE mocha 10→8.1.3** (npm picks the old version predating the vulnerable transitive). Forcing breaking changes to the build/test pillars to silence dev-server/dev-UI advisories the project never exercises (headless one-shot build/test; no `esbuild serve`, no Vitest UI server) trades a verified pipeline for nothing. (d) **Decision: accept dev-only + document** (`docs/SECURITY-AUDIT.md`: decision, four-way "none ship" evidence, per-advisory table, why-not-fix, when-to-revisit). A deliberate toolchain upgrade to clear them the RIGHT way (bump + re-run the full matrix, not `--force`) is a separate future maintenance session (backlog). **The one trigger that flips this:** if a runtime `dependency` is ever added, re-evaluate immediately — a vulnerable runtime dep WOULD bundle and ship. | Any `npm audit` / dependency-security posture call on a bundled artifact (VS Code extension, web bundle): check what actually ships (the bundle, not `node_modules`) before treating an advisory as user-facing; parse `--json`, never the exit code; treat `npm audit fix --force` as suspect (it can downgrade/break); accept-and-document dev-only vulns, do real upgrades as their own verified pass. Re-evaluate the instant a runtime `dependency` is introduced. |

(c) **Word expansion must be Unicode-aware:** `isWordChar = /[A-Za-z0-9_]/` split accented prose (`café` + bare-cursor bold → `**caf**é`), diverging from the project's own `language-configuration.json` `wordPattern` and the editor's word notion; fix is `/[\p{L}\p{N}_]/u`. (d) **Faithful keybinding-scope tests use EXACT equality, not substring:** `binding.when?.includes("editorLangId == quarto")` stays green even if the clause is broadened to `… || editorLangId == markdown` (a real scope regression) — assert `strictEqual(binding.when, "editorTextFocus && editorLangId == quarto")` (gate d). (e) **Review yield:** a 5-lens / 3-refuter refute-by-default Workflow raised 11, confirmed **3** (b/c/d above), refuted 8 (incl. (d) **Mid-line HTML comments are a `scanRegions` whole-line gap** (a comment opening `text <!--` or closing `--> $x$` mid-line is mis-handled) — confirmed real but DEFERRED + documented (shared-model, also affects `refs`; a cross-cutting fix needs its own TDD pass, FM #18 / no cross-module refactor without plan mode). Attribute in `NOTICE` (licensing hard gate). This is a webview **asset**, NOT a bundled runtime `dependency`, so it does NOT trip Learning #20 (`.vsix` 30 KB→368 KB; `npm audit` unchanged). | 24 | **Phase 6d spike + plan — resolving the YAML-schema dragon, and a planning-session decomposition (Session 17, planning-only, no code).** The deliverable was a plan: `docs/planning/2026-06-28-phase-6d-yaml-completion-plan.md`. Load-bearing findings the executor inherits: (a) **The schema source is MIT — it ships in the Quarto CLI, not the AGPL extension.** The completion data (option names, descriptions, value enums, jupyter-vs-knitr `tags.engine`) lives in `<quarto-share>/editor/tools/yaml/yaml-intelligence-resources.json` (97 cell-option + 414 front-matter key names), under the MIT CLI share dir (`COPYING.md`/`COPYRIGHT` = MIT Posit, no carve-out for `schema/` or `editor/tools/yaml/`). This is the Learning #1 CLI-vs-extension line: the AGPL part is the *extension*; the *CLI* (which the extension already depends on) is MIT. (b) **Pick the completion-shaped artifact, not the validation one — PROBE before choosing.** The standard `<share>/schema/json-schemas.json` (JSON Schema draft-2020-12, `$defs` keyed by *type names*) is for **validation** and lacks the flat key-grouping/`tags.engine`/per-key descriptions completion needs; `yaml-intelligence-resources.json` is Quarto's own pre-parsed completion resource (the right source). A sub-agent that was supposed to resolve this returned a **degenerate stub** — I closed the gap with a 1-command probe myself, and it **flipped** the other skeptic's durability-based preference. Lesson: when a delegated finding is load-bearing and an agent returns empty/garbage, verify it yourself before enshrining a recommendation. (c) **For data that drifts with the user's tool version, prefer RUNTIME-READ over vendoring.** Runtime-read the user's own install: zero version drift (a 1.4 user reads 1.4's schema, a 1.9 user reads 1.9's), +0 `.vsix` bytes, and **no redistribution → no NOTICE** (vendoring would trigger MIT attribution + a per-release re-distill+sha256 treadmill — Learning #20/#23 — for data meant to track the CLI). Resolve the share dir via `quarto --paths` — but it is **UNDOCUMENTED** (absent from `quarto --help`): parse it defensively (last line / the existing dir ending in `share`), re-verify on upgrade, exactly like the `Browse at`/`Output created` stderr markers (Learnings #4/#8/#11). Ship a **tiny curated static fallback** (never "no fallback"): completion-only use, never-throw → fewer suggestions, never a false "unknown key" (Learning #16 robustness). (d) **A "LARGE 🐉" phase is usually several capabilities — decompose it, don't slice one thing N ways.** An adversarial scope check **refuted** "6d is one capability": it varies on two independent cost axes — the **position detector** (cell-option detection reuses `findAllCells`; front-matter detection needs NEW model surface — `scanRegions` deliberately skips front matter, Gap #1) and the **schema subset / value-interpreter** (KEY completion just lists `name`s; VALUE completion must interpret the DSL `schema` field; nested/format-conditional needs the recursive `resolveRef`/`super` walk + `format-aliases`). So the plan is **5 vertical slices one-context-per-session**: cell-option KEY (smallest, ship first) → cell-option VALUE → schema reader (the dragon slice, isolated) → front-matter KEY → front-matter VALUE → (optional) nested. Never bundle front-matter+cell or key+value; never build the pure schema core as a standalone no-UI session (forbidden horizontal layer, FM #25); grow the core just-enough per slice. (e) **The inverted provider-gating trap:** the new YAML provider is the **complement** of the `@` cross-ref/citation providers — they share the whole-doc `{language:"quarto"}` selector and gate to prose via `isReferenceableLine`; the YAML provider must fire ONLY in front matter + `#|`/`//|` cell-option lines and return `undefined` elsewhere, so every provider-touching slice needs a no-cross-pollination regression test (no YAML items at a prose position; no `@` items in front matter). | Starting any Phase 6d slice (read the plan §6; start with **6d-1**). Any feature that needs a tool's schema/config data: check if it ships in that tool's MIT distribution (vs an AGPL companion); probe which artifact carries the data SHAPE you need; prefer runtime-read for version-drifting data (defensive parse of any undocumented resolver + curated fallback). Any "LARGE 🐉" phase: run a scope refutation before assuming it's one session — decompose along the position-detector × schema-subset axes, slice vertical one-context-per-session. Any new completion provider on the shared `{language:"quarto"}` selector: gate to its region and regression-test no cross-pollination. When a delegated agent returns a degenerate/empty load-bearing result, verify it firsthand before trusting the synthesis. |

| 25 | **Phase 6d-1 — `#|` cell-option KEY completion, the first 6d slice (Session 18).** Executed the smallest 6d slice (plan §6) strict-TDD/vertical, then hardened it with the per-phase adversarial review. (a) **The shape held: pure detection + pure data + thin adapter.** `findCellOptionLines` is a **view over the shared `findAllCells`** (Learning #14 — never a 2nd scanner; only interior lines of executable cells are examined, so a `#\|` in prose / a non-executable ```` ```python ```` block / a fence line is never reported, with a break-revert-proven skip-region agreement test). `core/yaml-context.ts` `completionContextAt(text, offset)` is the pure position discriminator (returns a `cell-option-key` context or `null`); `core/yaml-schema.ts` holds the curated fallback; `providers/yaml.ts` is the thin adapter. The curated `CURATED_CELL_OPTIONS` (~18) uses **names probed from the live schema** (uncopyrightable facts) with **our own descriptions** → license-clean, and is the *permanent* fallback 6d-3 degrades to, not throwaway. (b) **The inverted provider-gating contract (plan §4.3) is enforced by `completionContextAt` returning `null` everywhere except a key slot** — so the YAML provider naturally yields no items in prose/code/value/front matter while the `@` providers still fire in prose. **The faithfulness proof is the RED baseline itself:** the no-cross-pollination integration tests were GREEN *before* the provider existed (trivially, no items) and STAYED green after wiring it (proving no leak), while the positive test flipped RED→GREEN via the same `detail === "Quarto cell option"` discriminator — positive + negative controls in one transition (Learning #16d). (c) **Match the tool's ACTUAL syntax pattern, verified against its installed source — don't hand-write a plausible regex.** The adversarial review (5 lenses, refute-by-default, 3 refuters/finding: 8 raised → 3 confirmed/5 refuted) caught BOTH directions of a fidelity bug in my first `CELL_OPTION_PREFIX`: it **over-detected indented `#\|`** (Quarto anchors the directive at column 0 — `/^#\s*\| ?/`, an indented `#\|` is code) AND **under-detected `# \|`** (Quarto's `\s*` allows whitespace between the comment char and the pipe). Both fixed by aligning the regex to Quarto's real pattern (`/^(#\|\/\/)([ \t]*)\|([ \t]*)(.*)$/`), confirmed against `/Applications/quarto/.../web-worker.js` `optionCommentPattern` + an empirical `quarto render`. (d) **A "skip-region agreement" test for a cell-INTERIOR view must embed a REAL cell inside the skip region** — a fenceless fixture passes for the trivial "no cell" reason (it never reaches skip-region logic), an over-claim the review caught and I proved by break-revert (disabling the front-matter/comment skip turns the faithful form RED, the fenceless form stays GREEN). (e) **Contained-scope discipline:** the review's refuted findings were correctly out of scope — non-contiguous-`#\|` over-detection (benign, not §4.3), prefix/language-agnostic matching (per the plan contract — `#\|` in an ojs cell still completes), a missing front-matter `@`-suppression test (behavior already guarded by the 6b/6c in-cell suites), and the `refs.ts` `CELL_LABEL_OPTION` duplication (its own leading-`\s*` has the same latent indent looseness, but consolidation is the deferred 6d-2 follow-up — a cross-module change, FM #18, not this slice). | Any Phase 6d slice (6d-2 next: cell-option VALUE — extend `completionContextAt` past the colon + curated `values`; reuse this pure-detection + pure-data + thin-adapter split and the inverted-gating regression). Any detector of a tool's syntax: match the tool's ACTUAL pattern verified against its installed source, not a hand-written guess (re-verify on Quarto upgrade — add `optionCommentPattern` to the marker list, Learnings #4/#8/#11). Any "agreement"/skip-region test for a region-interior view: embed a real instance inside the skip region and break-revert it. | 26 | **Phase 6d-2 — `#|` cell-option VALUE completion, the second 6d slice (Session 19).** Grew 6d-1's three-piece split (pure detection + pure data + thin adapter) to value completion, strict-TDD/vertical with a checkpoint commit per layer, then the per-phase adversarial review. (a) **The slice extends, it does not rebuild.** `CellOptionLine` gained a `valueSlot` span computed in the SAME `slotsOf` pass as `keySlot` (model.ts) — no new scanner (Learning #14 holds); `completionContextAt` gained a `cell-option-value` branch returning `parentPath:[keyName]` (the key being valued, plan §5.2) and a `replaceRange` over the whole value token; `CURATED_CELL_OPTIONS` gained grounded `values`; `providers/yaml.ts` gained a value path. Adding `valueSlot` to the returned object **broke the 4 existing `.toEqual` shape-lock tests** — expected and mechanical (deep-equality rejects the new field); update them as shape locks, don't treat as behavior. (b) **Value completion must emit VALID YAML on accept.** A `:` trigger fires right after the colon with no space (`#| echo:`), so the provider prepends a space when the value slot **abuts the colon** (`lineText[startCol-1] === ":"`) → `#| echo: true`, not `#| echo:true`. Keep `filterText` = the bare value (no leading space) so fuzzy matching still works; only `insertText` carries the space. Detect "needs space" in the thin adapter (it has the live line), not the pure core. (c) **Ground enum values against the installed schema AND pin them with EXACT-equality tests — a `toContain` spot-check hides a transcription error.** The review (5 lenses, 3 refuters: 4 raised → 4 confirmed/0 refuted) caught that my curated `page-column` enum had **17 of 18 values** — I dropped the base `page-inset` transcribing my own correct probe output (the Learning #25 trap recurring: had the grounded data, fumbled the copy). My `column` test used `toContain` on 4 cherry-picked values (unlike its siblings' `toEqual`), so it sailed past the omission. Fix: restore the value + tighten to full-enum `toEqual`. **Every curated enum gets an exact-equality test, never a spot-check.** (d) **A value-token detector must stop at an unquoted inline comment.** `slotsOf` first swallowed a trailing `  # comment` into the value span, so values popped while editing the comment and accepting one **clobbered** it (the replaceRange covered the comment). Fix: terminate the value at a `#` that is at the value start or whitespace-preceded (YAML comment rule); leave quoted scalars intact (quote-aware parsing deferred). Both review fixes were TDD'd (RED before GREEN) and the `page-inset` one re-verified firsthand against `definitions.yml`. (e) **In-memory docs are the clean way to integration-test edge cases** — `openTextDocument({language:"quarto", content})` + `executeCompletionItemProvider` exercises the real registered provider without a fixture file or render concern (proven: the positive value cases flipped RED→GREEN on in-memory docs, so the provider genuinely fires on them). | Any Phase 6d value slice (6d-5 front-matter VALUE reuses this; 6d-3 schema reader replaces the curated source but keeps `values`/`valueSlot`/the value branch). Any completion that inserts after a separator (`:`): prepend the separator's required whitespace in `insertText` (keep `filterText` bare) when the slot abuts it, decided in the adapter. **Any curated/derived enum: ground it against the tool's installed source AND assert it with exact full-set equality — a `toContain` spot-check will not catch a dropped/extra value (Learnings #25/#16d, now recurred).** Any value-token span over a line that may carry a trailing comment: terminate at the unquoted comment start. Extending a returned data shape: expect `.toEqual` shape-lock tests to break — update them mechanically, they are not behavior regressions. Use in-memory `openTextDocument` docs to integration-test provider edge cases without a fixture. The `refs.ts`↔`model.ts` cell-option-prefix consolidation remains a BACKLOG follow-up (6d-2 did NOT route refs' `label:` through `findCellOptionLines` — that cross-module change stays its own pass, FM #18). |

Re-verify per-format facts (`getFormatSchema`, `format-aliases.yml`, a field's `tags.formats`) in the installed schema on a Quarto upgrade (Learnings #4/#8/#11/#25/#27/#32/#40/#41). |

Run the refutation on the tempting *shortcut* (it's free), not only on the inherited *difficulty* (it's a dragon). Have agents re-derive the plan's numbers firsthand, not just accept the author's — the Session-33 grounding-Workflow pattern earns its keep on counts, not only on the yes/no verdict. | 44 | **Phase 6d-6+ (b2-iii-key) — deep-nested per-format option KEY completion, the first b2-iii slice, executed across two session instances after a ghost-session gap (Session 37).** Implemented the Session-36 plan strict-TDD, 4 vertical layers (detector — a prior instance — → object-property resolver+reader → curated fallback → integration verification), checkpoint-commit each, then the per-phase adversarial review. (a) **🔑 Recovering from a ghost session (FM #14) is exactly what checkpoint-commit discipline is FOR.** Orientation found a `SESSION_NOTES.md` stub claiming "Session 37 IN PROGRESS" with only ONE commit behind it (the detector layer) and no close-out — a prior instance had checkpoint-committed layer 1 cleanly, then vanished. Nothing was lost: `npm test` was green, the working tree was clean, and the plan's own §6 layer breakdown told the continuing instance exactly what remained (resolver → curated → integration). The per-layer checkpoint-commit rule (`SESSION_RUNNER.md` §Vertical Slice Sessions gate b) is not just a verifiability aid — it is the RECOVERABILITY mechanism a crashed/lost session depends on. (b) **🔑 Firsthand grounding beats even a well-grounded plan's own pseudocode — mirror the FULL algorithm in a throwaway script and diff to the exact grounded fact before writing any TypeScript.** The Session-36 plan's §5.2 named three unwrap forms (`anyOf`/`ref`/`maybeArrayOf`) for the object-property resolver. (c) **🔑 THE REVIEW'S HEADLINE CATCH: unwrapping `arrayOf` the same as `maybeArrayOf` is a category error — an UNCONDITIONAL array and an OPTIONAL array are not the same completion shape.** `maybeArrayOf` means "may be a single object OR an array" — a bare mapping IS valid, so listing the wrapped object's properties as completion keys is correct. A bare `arrayOf` (no anyOf arm permitting a lone object — e.g. `other-links`, and a second instance the review's own scope missed but firsthand re-verification caught, `filters`) means the value can ONLY be a `-`-prefixed sequence; offering its array element's properties as MAPPING keys produces schema-invalid completions (`other-links:\n  text: …` instead of `other-links:\n  - text: …`). When writing a new type-narrowing check, grep the same file for how equivalent checks are already guarded. (e) **A confirmed, real finding can still be correctly OUT OF SCOPE for the current slice — refute it as "belongs elsewhere," not "not real."** The review also confirmed two genuine defects that were correctly refuted FOR b2-iii-key specifically because they're pre-existing and unrelated to object-children resolution: `valuesOfSchema` doesn't unwrap the `{boolean:{description,default}}` DSL form (a real gap, but squarely b2-iii-value's job — the value-side slice — not this KEY slice's); and `collectFields`'s first-occurrence-wins name dedup silently drops Quarto's richer `copyright` definition in favor of a poorer same-named one (a pre-existing bug in shared front-matter key collection, unrelated to deep nesting at all). Both recorded in `BACKLOG.md` for their own future sessions rather than fixed here (SAFEGUARDS: no cross-module refactor without plan mode) or silently dropped. (f) **1st non-clean review after a 13-session (Sessions 25–35 + the b2 plan) clean-review streak** — a 5-lens/3-refuter Workflow (23 agents, ~3.06M tokens) raised 6, confirmed 3 (all fixed test-first, each break-revert-proven — including the `arrayOf` fix against the REAL schema, reproducing the literal 5-key leak `href`/`icon`/`rel`/`target`/`text`); one refuted finding (a weak `.includes()` gate-d assertion where the SAME describe block's sibling test already used exact-set) was applied anyway as a cheap, in-scope, low-risk nicety, consistent with the project's established "refuted but worthwhile" pattern. 482 unit (+9) / 165 integration (+7); clean 33-file `.vsix`; §3.3 core stays `vscode`-free. | Recovering from a ghost session: trust the last clean checkpoint commit + the plan's layer breakdown, don't re-derive from scratch. Any object/array-unwrapping resolver: mirror the FULL intended algorithm in a throwaway script against the REAL data and diff to the plan's own grounded facts BEFORE coding — a plan's pseudocode can under-specify even when its counts are correct. Any schema-wrapper unwrap that treats several DSL forms as interchangeable: verify they produce the same OUTPUT SHAPE (mapping keys vs. sequence items), not just that they "eventually resolve" — an unconditional array is not an optional array. Any new type-narrowing check (`typeof x === "object"`): grep the same file for how sibling checks already guard against arrays, and match that precedent. Any review finding that is real but touches a DIFFERENT capability (a different slice, a pre-existing shared-code bug): refute it for THIS slice's scope while recording it in BACKLOG for its own session — don't silently drop it and don't fix it out of scope. Any refuted-but-cheap test-quality suggestion, applied for free when consistent with an existing sibling test in the same file. The next b2-iii-key slices: **b2-iii-value** ground+fix the `valuesOfSchema` `{boolean:{...}}}` gap alongside the two already-known forms (`{enum:{values}}}` + `{tags,schema}}}`); **b2-iii-deep** (depth-4 + `super`) remains deferred. (a) **A sibling `../methodology` checkout already existed** (not obvious without checking) with `origin`→`rmsharp/methodology` (fork) and `upstream`→`KJ5HST/methodology` (canonical, the URL the operator gave) — `git fetch` on both showed local `main` already even with `upstream/main` (0 commits behind), so "using https://github.com/KJ5HST/methodology" resolved to **`../methodology/bin/sync .`** from that already-current checkout, not a fresh clone (`bin/sync … --source=github` is the fallback ONLY when no sibling checkout exists). Always check for a sibling methodology checkout (`ls ..`, check remotes) before assuming a GitHub fetch is needed. (b) **`bin/status .` before syncing is the right first move** — it showed zero `locally modified` files (every tracked file was either `current` or cleanly `N versions behind`), so the sync was safe to run for-real with no `--force` and no drift-reconciliation needed; had any file shown `locally modified`, the correct move would have been extracting the customization into CLAUDE.md's Adaptations section first (this project already does this correctly — all project-specific rules and all 44 prior Learnings live here, never in the synced files themselves). (c) **The sync pulled a NEW receipt discipline (methodology v3.5): `HANDOFFS.md`**, a durable, git-committed, machine-checkable append-only ledger of one `handoff` block per session (claimed `status: pending` at Phase 1B, completed `status: complete` at Phase 3D) — distinct from the transient `SESSION_NOTES.md` (overwritten every session) and the cumulative `CHANGELOG.md` (action-level, not session-level). It was a `seed` (create-if-absent, never overwritten after) so `bin/sync` created it fresh with a seed-sentinel; this session wrote the FIRST real receipt, deleting the sentinel. **Going forward, every session's Phase 1B must open a `status: pending` stub in `HANDOFFS.md` (this session skipped that, since the task began before the sync existed) and Phase 3D must close it to `status: complete`; Phase 0 step 6 now ALSO reconciles `HANDOFFS.md`'s frontier the same way it reconciles `CHANGELOG.md`'s.** (d) **`SESSION_RUNNER.md`/`SAFEGUARDS.md` also gained two OPTIONAL, non-shipped mechanisms this session did not wire up** (both explicitly "recommendation, not a shipped mechanism"): a `pre-commit` hook that refuses a commit changing tracked content unless `CHANGELOG.md` is co-staged (`.githooks/pre-commit`, canonical-only, opt-in via `git config core.hooksPath .githooks`), and a harness session-end hook idea that checks `HANDOFFS.md` for a trailing `status: pending` before letting a turn end. Neither was set up here — BACKLOG if the operator wants them. (e) **Verification for a pure methodology-file sync is `npm run compile` (clean, since zero `src/**` files changed) + `python3 methodology_dashboard.py` (ran clean, 78/100 health, the 1 CRITICAL flag is the pre-existing dev-only npm-audit posture from Learning #20, unrelated) — no unit-test gate applies** (CLAUDE.md's TDD mandate is scoped to this project's OWN implementation logic; the synced methodology tooling is upstream-owned, not something this project authors or unit-tests). | Any future "update/sync the methodology" request: check for a sibling `methodology/` checkout first (`ls ..`, `git remote -v` inside it) before reaching for `--source=github`; run `bin/status .` before `bin/sync .` to confirm no local drift (if drift exists, extract the customization to CLAUDE.md's Adaptations section first, exactly as this project already does for all 44+ Learnings and the TDD mandate). **Starting with the next session:** Phase 1B must open a `status: pending` `HANDOFFS.md` stub alongside the `SESSION_NOTES.md` claim-stub, and Phase 3D must close it to `status: complete` with all six Minimum Handoff Requirements — this is now a structural gate, not optional, and Phase 0 step 6 will catch a skipped one at the next Orient. A pure docs/tooling-only sync needs no unit tests (out of the TDD mandate's scope) but still needs `npm run compile` + the dashboard run as its normal verification. |
| 47 | **Phase 6d-6+ (b2-iii-value) — deep-nested per-format option VALUE completion, the b2-iii-key trace-first claim held again, and Quarto's schema DSL represents "the same conceptual thing" in at least THREE incompatible JSON shapes depending on where it's defined (Session 40).** The detector and provider needed ZERO changes (confirmed by reading, then locked with a break-revert-proven detector test for the new 4-element value-context path `["format",fmt,opt,key]`) — all real work was 3 small extensions to `valuesOfSchema` in `core/yaml-schema.ts`. All three gaps were confirmed by grounding a Python probe against the REAL installed 1.7.33 `yaml-intelligence-resources.json` BEFORE writing any TypeScript (never trust that "it's just a boolean" collapses to one shape). (c) **A pre-existing BACKLOG item marked "refuted as latent, unreachable in 1.7.33" (Session 20, cell-options-only framing: "no cell option refs an object-form enum") turned out to be load-bearing once the SAME function was asked to resolve a DOCUMENT option's sub-property** (`html-math-method.method`, reached only via b2-iii's deep-nesting path) — a "latent/unreachable" verdict is scoped to the call sites checked at the time, not a permanent property of the code; re-verify it before reusing the verdict in a new context. (d) **Test-only detector claims still need a break-revert to prove they discriminate, even when the change is "just a new test."** Adding the 4-element `frontmatter-value` shape-lock test to `yaml-context.test.ts` needed zero production code — but gating the value branch to `parentPath.length < 3` (a targeted, precise break) reds ONLY that test while the existing 3-element b2-ii value test stays green, proving the lock is real and not vacuously true. (e) **Off-by-one column arithmetic is the recurring cost of hand-computing integration-test positions from a `join("\n")` literal** — every position bug this session was a miscounted `startCol`/`endCol` from mis-counting a hyphenated key's character length (`render-on-save` = 14 chars, not 13; `chapters` = 8, not 7) or the cursor offset within a partial token (`"fa|lse"` = start+2, not start+3); a `python3 -c "print(len(...))"` one-liner against the exact literal caught every instance immediately.
